# Phase 4 Decision Log

> D-number convention continues from `docs/phase-3/LOG.md`. Accepted parity deviations (4.3.1) and skipped-with-reason tasks (Definition of Done) get logged here.

|---|---|---|---|
| D358 | 2026-08-26 | **P6.4 / 4.4.5 final pass — CLOSED. 45-run parity board, four-FAIL triage, one harness fix, and a bench run whose M1a channel is VOID for the third time.** Builds: `npx tsc --noEmit` rc=0 from `showcase/`, `showcase` build rc=0, bench build rc=0. **Parity: all 43 registered chart kinds at mandated density plus `ring` n=4 and `sunburst` n=33 = 45 runs; 41 PASS / 4 FAIL as captured, 42 PASS / 3 known-non-parity after the harness fix.** **All three 4.4.1 baseline gate failures resolved:** `pie` n=1000 (unowned since 2026-08-21, hover-30/70 ≈3.18%) → 0.0138/0.0000/0.0000/0.0007 PASS; `choropleth` hover-30 1.5345% → **0.0000% with both tooltips asserting** at n=100; `heatmap` 0.5196/0.5218/0.5945% → 0.3466/0.3533/0.4256%. `sankey`, deferred out of the baseline at 2.84/1.94/1.43%, → 0.3835/0.2782/0.1323% at n=33. **FAIL triage, each with its own control.** (a) **`arealoading` = HARNESS GAP, fixed.** All four captures PASSED (≤0.0640%); the FAIL was the tooltip assertion firing **symmetrically on A and B**. `--self-test` scores **0.0000% on all four and still reports `overall: FAIL`** — the instrument fails against itself. A skeleton has no data, so neither impl renders a tooltip. `arealoading`+`barloading` added to `TOOLTIPLESS_CHARTS` (`qa/screenshot.mjs`); **pixel gate untouched**. Re-gate PASS. (b) **`barloading` = not a parity gate by prior design** (its own scenario header + D341); settled **improved 4.5259%→1.8975%**; self-test 0.0000%, so the hovers are clean signal and are **new information D341 lacked** — settled→hover movement **bklit 3.05% vs migrated 16.35%**, i.e. the skeleton animations diverge over TIME, not geometry. (c) **`ring` n=1000 = invalid instrument (D335), now corroborated structurally:** bklit holds **962 of 975** transformed groups at `scale(0)` vs migrated's 40 at exactly `scale(1)`, neither exceeding 1.0 — so the 0.72% is not a hover-scale mismatch but which arcs are visible in a stagger that never completes; `ring` n=4 (gate of record) PASSES. **Against D335: today's self-test *passed* (max 0.0935%) where D335's failed — the instrument is marginal, not reliably self-failing.** (d) **`candletween` = pixel-invalid by design (D330 §2):** self-test **FAILS at 0.1883%** against a 0.1% floor and the two runs disagree (1.3619 vs 1.4408); the k4 probe reads migrated at **1800 ms / `cubic-bezier(0.85,0,0.15,1)` / 64 frames / delays 0→21.6 ms** (K4's contract) against bklit **`animCount: 0`**, framer on rAF, so the probe reads one side by construction. **DOM probes:** `sb-chrome-probe` PASS on all four states and all four presence assertions; `k4-tween-probe`/`reveal-probe` are readback dumps with no verdicts (sankey 33 flows, dur 1100, delays 0→561.3 ms). **BENCH: 10 exclusive paired cells** (line/area/composed/scatter n=1000, bar n=100), process table asserted empty first. **M1a VOID — third consecutive run (P6.1, P6.2, P6.4):** the `bklit` control moved **+8.3%** (scatter 128.2→138.9), **+12.3%** (area) and **+8.8%** (line) past the ±8% threshold. **No M1a claim is made from this run, and M1a should not gate anything in Phase 5 without a fresh in-run control.** M1b/M1c/M3a/M3c controls held (≤4.1%) and are valid; heap controls moved ≤5.8%, valid only for large gaps. On the valid channels migrated is at or ahead everywhere but `composed`: script cost **−77% composed / −76% scatter / −63% bar**, per-move script lower on all five, heap **12.2→6.8 MB** composed and **8.1→5.3 MB** scatter. **Two regressions stand, both `composed`, both carried from P6.2 not new:** M1b +27% and M3a ~1.5× — and bklit's composed M3a control of 21.5 ms is the outlier LOW against a 29–33 ms cluster on both impls everywhere else. | The 4.4.5 charter is a three-part gate (build + bench + ALL-chart spot-check vs the 4.4.1 baseline) and each part was run to its own evidentiary standard rather than to a green light. D331 governed the triage: every FAIL was pushed through a control before being labelled, which is what separated the one genuine harness defect (`arealoading`) from three fixtures already ruled non-parity, and what forced the honest `ring` correction against D335. The harness fix was deliberately confined to the tooltip assertion — widening `TOOLTIPLESS_CHARTS` cannot mask a pixel regression, whereas touching the gate or `includeAA` would have invalidated every historical baseline (D337). D273 governed the bench: the control channel is read FIRST and a channel whose control moved is declared void rather than quietly reported, which is why the strong M1c/heap results are claimed and the flattering M1a numbers are not. |
| D357 | 2026-08-26 | **D340 (bottom-edge gridline) formally SKIPPED-WITH-REASON for the Definition of Done, not silently dropped.** bklit erases the plot-floor gridline by masking; migrated's grid is rendered natively by TanStack as `.ts-chart__grid`, and there is no `<g mask>` to hang an equivalent erasure on. Every way to reproduce it either replaces native grid rendering with hand-rolled lines — directly against MAIN GOAL (b), TanStack-native backend — or hangs a bbox-dependent CSS hack off measured plot geometry. Measured severity: one hairline at the plot floor, **0.047%**, an order of magnitude under the 0.5%% gate, and stable across every run it has appeared in. | Recording the reason matters more than the outcome here. This is the one open visual delta in the phase, and the tempting close-out is to smuggle in a geometry-dependent CSS override at the end of the phase so the ledger reads clean. That trade is bad twice over: it buys a 0.047%% cosmetic win by taking on a fragile dependency on measured layout, and it does so in the final pass, where there is the least remaining gate coverage to catch what it breaks. Phase 5, if it revisits grid rendering, is the right place — a native TanStack grid affordance would make this a one-line fix instead of a hack. Deliberately deferred, with the number and the blocking reason on the record so the next phase inherits a decision rather than a mystery. |
| D356 | 2026-08-26 | **`centralize 5` executed (not re-routed): the 33 raw `dataset.bkmRevealed` sites across 9 chart files converged onto primitives in `internal/deferred-reveal.ts` — `isRevealed` / `markRevealed` / `clearRevealed` / `findRevealRoot` over a new `RevealRoot = HTMLElement \| SVGElement` type — and in doing so uncovered a latent dead-guard class: `.ts-chart__marks` is an `SVGGElement`, so every `marksGroup instanceof HTMLElement` guard inside `runDeferredReveal` was permanently false and the stamp was never written. Fixing the type meant the latch started working for the first time, which in turn required a new epoch-advance `clearRevealed` before the guard so a heatmap epoch bump is not vetoed by the previous epoch's now-real stamp. Gates: `tsc rc=0`, `bench build rc=0`, 10-chart QA board all PASS with heatmap `0.4277/0.3466/0.3533/0.4256` matching D250's F3 closing gate and sunburst `0.0599` matching D281 at both 27 and 33. **D331 control run and it came back NEGATIVE:** with `isRevealed` stubbed to `return false` and `markRevealed` stubbed to a no-op — the latch fully disabled, reveals free to re-fire on every re-render — line/heatmap/sunburst reproduced all twelve numbers identical to four decimals. | Two things must be said plainly rather than dressed up. First, the refactor is not a pure refactor: it changed behaviour, because the code it centralized had never actually run. `checkRevealGuard` existed and was almost unused, and the call sites show why — it reads and stamps in one step while nearly every chart must read first and stamp on a specific branch, so the centralization had to be a set of primitives, not one combined function. Second, the QA board proving PASS proves nothing about this subsystem: the D331 control establishes the gate cannot see the reveal latch at all, in either direction. The PASS is evidence that nothing else regressed, and is not evidence that the latch works. Per D331 a gate that cannot detect the presence it seeks is not evidence, and the honest record is that the latch fix is currently unverified by the harness — the reveal is a sub-frame WAAPI transient and the settled screenshot is taken after it completes, so the harness is blind by construction, not by threshold. |
| **D355** | 2026-08-26 | **The two lead rulings CH17 Part 3 left open are RULED, and both flags turned out to be wrong about their own subject.** **(a) The 7 `*CssVars` — RESTORE, not ACCEPT.** The ledger described them as an architectural swap of DOC-4's shape: "legacy generated CSS custom properties from TS constants; migrated declares them in `styles.css`". **Both halves are false.** Legacy generates no custom property — `ringCssVars` et al are plain TS objects whose VALUES are `var(--…)` reference *strings*, consumed as `fill={ringCssVars.ringBackground}` (`ring.tsx:189`, `ring-chart.tsx:364`). And migrated's `styles.css` does not declare the underlying `--chart-*` properties either; the host app's theme does, **in both implementations** (`grep -n -- '--chart-1:' styles.css` → no hits). Nothing was swapped: migrated inlined the literal strings at their use sites (`ring-chart.tsx:71`) and dropped the public maps. That is a plain public-API gap under MAIN GOAL (c), not a doctrine. Restored in a new `internal/css-var-maps.ts` and barrel-exported: `chartScaleCssVars`, `choroplethCssVars`, `heatmapCssVars` (with legacy's `@deprecated` notice carried verbatim), `pieCssVars`, `radarCssVars`, `ringCssVars`, `sankeyCssVars`, plus the two direct dependencies `CHART_SCALE_VARS` / `ChartScaleVars` and `defaultChoroplethColors`. **Chart internals deliberately NOT rewired to read from the maps.** **(b) `TooltipContentProps` — the flag is STALE, there is no collision.** The ledger said "legacy exports a general tooltip type; migrated's is sankey-local; exporting it as-is could be wrong". P5.2 had already landed a `TooltipContentProps` at `internal/tooltip-components.tsx:579` **byte-identical to legacy's** `tooltip/tooltip-content.tsx:12` (`title?`, `rows: TooltipRow[]`, `children?`), and the barrel had already disambiguated the sankey-local one as `SankeyTooltipContentProps` (`index.ts:440`, with a comment saying so). The only real gap was that the barrel shipped the COMPONENT `TooltipContent` and not its props type, which legacy exports (`index.ts:598`). One line. **Fixed alongside, same class:** legacy exports all four `default*Colors`; migrated exported `defaultPieColors` and `defaultRingColors` and kept `DEFAULT_RADAR_COLORS`/`DEFAULT_SCATTER_COLORS` private — values verified identical to `radar-context.tsx:23` and `chart-context.tsx:55`. Now aliased out of the barrel. **Gates:** `TSC_EXIT=0`, `BUILD_EXIT=0`; `scatter` n=1000 PASS (0.4510/0.0000/0.0000/0.2616), `radar` n=6 PASS (0.0141/0.0128/0.0888/0.0141). | **Per D331 those two gates are NOT evidence for this change and are not offered as such.** The edit is export-only plus two `export` keywords; a pixel gate on it is a definitional no-op and no control could make it fail. They were run for exactly one reason — to prove the two chart files I typed in are not broken — and that is all they prove. The substantive verification here is textual: legacy definition read side-by-side with the restored one, key by key. **The wider finding is about the ledger, not the symbols.** Both flags were raised in good faith and both mis-stated their subject in the same direction — each assumed a deliberate architectural decision behind what was, in fact, an omission. Bucket 2's justification was invented from the fact that a `styles.css` exists; Bucket 3's from a name match with a sankey-local type, without checking whether the general one had since landed. **The lesson for the remaining audit rows is that "ACCEPTED under a doctrine not yet written down" is a phrase to distrust**: if no doctrine names it, the likeliest explanation is that no decision was ever made. Bucket 4 (UNACCOUNTED) being zero is still correct — every symbol did have an owner — but having an owner is not the same as having been ruled on, and these two were the ledger's own flagged exceptions, so its zero survives. |

| **D348** | 2026-08-26 | **The QA harness's generic hover probe picked the WRONG `<svg>` for any migrated chart with an `xDomain`, and blamed the chart for it.** `qa/screenshot.mjs:458` took `page.locator("#chart-root svg").first()`. `line-chart.tsx:1045` renders a **0x0 clipPath-defs `<svg>` BEFORE the chart `<svg>`** whenever `needsBrushClip` (`!!xDomain`) is true, so `.first()` returned a 0x0 box and all three hover points landed on the container origin, off the plot. Result: `tooltipA=true tooltipB=false` on every hover row of `projectionxdomain` — a reported tooltip failure on a chart that hovers correctly. Fixed by taking the LARGEST `#chart-root svg` by area, which is exactly what the BRUSH capture path at `:397` already did — its own comment names this hazard verbatim ("impl-fragile: migrated hosts a 0x0 clipPath-defs svg before the chart svg"), which is why `brush` never showed the bug. | The instrument was wrong, not the subject. Diagnosed by driving playwright directly: zero focus callbacks reached `use-hover-chrome.ts`'s `handleFocusGroupChange`, and `document.querySelector("svg").getBoundingClientRect()` returned `{x:24,y:24,w:0,h:0}` — the probe was hovering the defs node. Confirmed by the fix: hover diffs on `projectionxdomain` fell 0.4219/0.4295/0.4043% -> 0.0360/0.0360/0.0907% and all three tooltip assertions flipped to true. Bklit puts its real `<svg>` first, so the A side always hovered correctly and the PIXEL gates still passed under the 0.5% bar — only the tooltip assertion caught it, and it pointed at the wrong impl. The three chart-specific probes at `:485/:520/:559` (funnel/heatmap/sankey) still use `querySelector("#chart-root svg")`; none of those charts sets `xDomain`, so they are unaffected and left alone. |
| **D347** | 2026-08-26 | **P6.2 item 1 — the time->pixel math for out-of-spec overlays is now ONE function, `internal/x-time-scale.ts`'s `timeToPixelX`, and consolidating it fixed two real parity defects in line-chart.** (a) line-chart carried **five** inline copies that recomputed the extent from `renderData` and merged the projection max UNCONDITIONALLY — i.e. they skipped the `xDomain` short-circuit the chart's own `timeExtent` applies. bklit has exactly ONE x scale (`time-series-chart-shell.tsx:284-301`: when `xDomain` is set the domain IS `xDomain` and `mergeProjectionXDomainMax` is deliberately SKIPPED) published as `useChartStable().xScale` and consumed unchanged by `line-series-terminal-marker.tsx:46` and `projection-line-end-marker.tsx:27`. `timeExtentRaw`/`timeExtent` were hoisted above the definition memo so all five sites read the same pair the spec's x domain gets. area-chart and composed-chart already read `timeExtent`; their four sites collapsed to one-liners. (b) All three charts' terminal-anchor memos read the **unfiltered `data` prop**; bklit's provider `data` IS `visiblePlotData` (`time-series-chart-shell.tsx:418`) and `<LineSeriesTerminalMarker>` anchors to `data.at(-1)` off that, so under a narrowed `xDomain` the marker belongs on the last point INSIDE the viewport. Switched to `renderData` in line/area/composed. Also centralized: `DATA_TWEEN_MS` -> `DEFAULT_Y_DOMAIN_TWEEN_MS` (chart-phase), inline `duration: 500` -> `SERIES_MARKER_ENTER_MS` (design-tokens, bklit `series-markers.tsx:103`), and `Y_DOMAIN_TWEEN_SKIP_THRESHOLD` added to chart-phase. A stale design-tokens comment claiming line/area/composed hold literal copies of `REVEAL_DURATION_MS`/`REVEAL_EASE_CSS` was corrected — they resolve through `enter-transition`'s `TWEEN_FALLBACK`. | No existing gate could see either defect: under every shipping scenario `xDomain` is unset, and then the five inline copies and `timeExtent` agree exactly. Per D331 a new fixture was built, `projectionxdomain` (the `projection` scenario + a narrowed `xDomain`), and it took **three** attempts to become evidence, all three failures being D337's blindness: pixelmatch at `threshold: 0.1, includeAA: false` discarded a 2px `1,4` dashed gradient stroke wholesale (control moved 0.009pp), then discarded a fat 10px SOLID stroke because a 6-point horizon made the misplaced geometry a ~15x25px stub (0.02pp). Only shrinking the viewport to 30% and lengthening the horizon to 600 points gave the misread overlay enough area to register. Final D331 control (pre-P6.2 inline math re-armed behind a `teLegacy` memo, `tsc` rc=0, bench app rebuilt): settled **0.3182%** vs **0.0723%** fixed — a 4.4x move, well clear of the 0.1% self-test determinism floor. Stated plainly: the control MOVES but does not TRIP the 0.5% gate, so this fixture is a detector, not a tripwire. |

(b) an 11 px horizontal inset, because bklit's skeleton uses `xOffset = bandWidth*0.15` (d3's `paddingInner 0.3 / paddingOuter 0.15`) while `.padding(barGap)` sets BOTH to 0.3 — measured bar runs `76,157,238,…` vs `87,166,245,…`, widths identical at 57 px on both sides. |

My resolver modelled one repo where the project has two, so it read *legacy* citations as *migrated* ones. The charters were right and I was wrong. This is the failure mode a mechanical check is *supposed* to have: it fails **loudly** and in the cheap direction (D310's distinction), costing a two-minute confirmation rather than a run. It is also the reason §4's warning matters — the check's value is entirely in what it rules OUT, never in what it appears to confirm. §9 SEPARATELY, P5.2 CAUGHT A BAD LOCATOR OF MINE FROM INSIDE THE RUN: I cited D228 as living in `docs/phase-4/LOG.md`; it is in `docs/phase-3/LOG.md:53`. The executor found it, reported it under the charter's own §6.7 wrong-locator clause, and continued — the defence clause working as designed, and a reminder that the pre-dispatch grep I am praising here is a filter, not a guarantee. It also missed a cross-phase citation precisely because my scan only checks `.ts`/`.tsx` paths, not `D###`->document claims. |

This closes T-D14 entirely and is the NINTH D272-shaped closure — but the blocker is not the one `tanstack.md` recorded, and the real one is stronger.** | **1. The recorded reason was a convention; the actual reason is a contract.** `tanstack.md` row 13 says *partial — "row-filtering app-owned (docs pattern)"*, i.e. TanStack's docs put the filter app-side. That framing makes it sound like a style preference a package could reasonably override. It is not. **2. The filtered rows have a SECOND consumer that `axis.viewport` cannot reach.** `line-chart.tsx:217-232` (and `area-chart.tsx:324` identically) implements a deliberate **split**, ported from bklit and commented as such at `:216` — *"bklit shell:265-277 visibleData; shell:341 yDomain on visible slice; shell:303-306 marks stay on full data (domain-clamp)"*: `visibleData` (the brushed subset) feeds **`resolveTimeSeriesYDomain` and nothing else**, while `renderData` (the FULL dataset) feeds every mark, the grid, the hover index and the tooltip. So brushing into a flat region **rescales Y to that region** while the marks stay on the full series and the x-scale clamps. **3. `axis.viewport` is architecturally the wrong shape for that split.** The native primitive maps a semantic viewport *while retaining the content domain* — `configured-scale.test.ts:98-119`: `viewport: { domain: [10,20] }` yields `scales.x.viewport.domain === [10,20]` alongside `scales.x.viewport.contentDomain === [0,30]`. It clips the **view**; it does not hand a narrower **row set** to an independent y-domain computation, and there is no surface on it that would. Swapping it in would leave the y-domain computed over all rows — **a visible parity break exactly where brushing is most used**, and one a `settled`-only gate on an unbrushed chart would never catch. **4. Therefore the row is closed as ACCEPT-custom, not deferred.** Nothing here is waiting on a bigger change: bklit's own behaviour is the split, migrated already reproduces it, and the native primitive addresses a different problem (viewport clamping) that migrated separately gets for free. **T-D14 is now fully closed** — rows 39/41/42/11 closed earlier, 25 NON-VIABLE, 26 + 27 LANDED (D291), 13 NON-VIABLE here. **5. Note the shape of the near-miss.** `tanstack.md`'s partial-viability note plus P3-13.md's restatement of it would have let a package open this row, find `axis.viewport` genuinely present and genuinely applicable to the *x* axis, and land it — the swap typechecks and the unbrushed render is identical. The thing that makes it wrong is one line of a comment three files away describing which consumer gets which array. **Both `filterDataByXDomain` and `createXAccessor` are already public (`index.ts:354`, P5.1's work), so the custom code is not an internal wart — it is deliberate API.** |

What this says about the class.** D304 split premise-rot into *dependency* and *locator*. This is a third kind — a **negative existence claim** ("X is never exported", "none of these have Y"). Root cause: the `fadeEdges` edge-fade mask was hoisted onto `g.ts-chart__marks` (the whole marks group) instead of the series paths. Re-scoping the three rules to `.ts-chart__line, .ts-chart__area` fixes both. `markers n=100` now PASSES on all 8 phases for the first time in the port; hover-30 `0.5570% → 0.3870%`.** | **§1 Every phase sat near the gate, which is the tell.** The reported chronic was a single-phase FAIL (`hover-30 0.5570%`), but the report showed `settled 0.3493`, `hover-50 0.3530`, `hover-70 0.4859`, and all three legend phases `0.4905–0.4914`. A defect that lands on one phase is a hover bug; a defect that lifts *every* phase to within a hair of the gate is a **render floor**, and hover-30 was merely the phase whose own noise pushed the floor over 0.5%. That reading is what redirected the investigation away from hover code. **§2 D213 applied: the diff PNG named the mechanism.** The red was concentrated at the left and right ends of the series band with grey (matching) through the middle — a horizontal-extent signature. Cropping `settled-a`/`settled-b` at 6× over `x∈[52,182]` showed bklit's dot band opaque edge to edge and migrated's fading to near-invisible; the right-edge crop showed migrated's dots gone entirely, exposing the dashed projection segment beneath. **§3 The mechanism, from computed style not source.** The ancestor-opacity probe returned `g.ts-chart__marks { mask: linear-gradient(to right, …) }` for both the first and last circle. bklit's `fade-edges.ts` is consumed by `line.tsx:294` (`line-gradient-${dataKey}`) and `area-gradient-defs.tsx` as the **stroke of that series' path** — `fadeEdges` is a per-series prop on `<Line>` (default `true`) and `<Area>` (default `false`), and dots carry a flat `oklch(0.374 0.01 67.558)` fill. Nothing in bklit ever fades a dot. **§4 The second, hidden defect.** A CSS mask resolves its percentage stops against the element's own fill-box. Measured on `markers n=100`: `.ts-chart__marks` = `x31 w738`, `.ts-chart__line` = `x40 w720`. The r=9 dots bulge the group box 9px past the line at each end, so every stop was mis-pinned ~6px, whereas the series box matches bklit's `viewportFadeGradientAttrs(innerWidth)` **exactly**. The re-scope therefore corrects the gradient geometry as well as its subject — and it is a provable no-op where the group holds only lines (plain `line` chart measures `marks == line == x40 w972`). **§5 The rule's own comment was the false premise.** It read *"Equivalent here: mask the marks group — its fill-box spans the same inner width."* Both halves were wrong, and it had survived every review because it stated its reasoning confidently and the gate was green on the charts that happen to have no dots. The replacement comment records the measured boxes and the bklit call sites so the shortcut cannot be re-derived. **§6 Reinforces D265 §5's new caveat (D297).** This is the fourth chronic in a row that turned out to be a real defect sitting quietly inside — or just outside — a passing gate. `markers 0.5570%` had been carried as "unowned chronic" across multiple sweeps on the strength of its stability. Stability meant only that the bug was deterministic. |

Post-fix probe: bklit value `103,131 122x46 fs 30px`, migrated `103,131 122x46 fs 30px`; bklit label `151,179 26x18 fs 12px`, migrated `151,179 26x18 fs 12px` — **identical rects, both impls**. **§4 D52 re-verified, not disturbed.** `leading-tight` is now a real utility, so styles.css:507-518's argument that it is DEAD at runtime (tailwind-merge deletes the earlier `leading-*` when the later `text-[…]` sets font-size) was re-checked against the live DOM rather than assumed: bklit's label computes line-height **18px = 12 × 1.5**, not × 1.25. The D52 port stands; the comment's "restoration recipe" is still the correct contingency. **§5 The doctrinal cost — D265 §5 needs a caveat.** "A suspicious number that matches history byte-for-byte is more trustworthy than a clean number that doesn't" was applied three times in the previous session (ring `0.0868`, gauge `0.1034 ×4`, pie matching D278). It validates the harness and the ceiling; **it says nothing about the performance of the code we are actually shipping.** I nearly reported "all migrated cells within ±10% of baseline" — a vacuous statement, since there are no migrated cells to be within anything. Caught only by checking which impls the results file actually contained. **§5 Consequence for the Definition of Done.** `migrated` IS benchable via single-cell mode, which has no impl allowlist: `node bench/run.mjs --chart <c> --impl migrated --n <n>`. But **no `migrated` baseline exists at HEAD `7d60a7f`**, and the tree has been dirty since Wave 1, so a true before/after for `migrated` can no longer be reconstructed for cells nobody captured at the time. **4.4.5's final bench compare must therefore be explicit about what it can and cannot prove**, and Wave 4+ should capture `migrated` cells per package rather than relying on `--all`. **§6 CONTROL CHANNEL — the D273 check, run before any conclusion.** `bklit` is untouched legacy, so it must not move. It did, modestly: four cells exceed ±8%, **all `m1a_mountToPaintMs`, all NEGATIVE** (area/100 −17.5%, scatter/100 −14.3%, bar/100 −13.4%, area/1000 −11.8%); worst control move overall **17.5%**; M1b and M3a controls held. This is a uniformly *faster* environment, not the mid-cell regime shift that voided the 2026-08-24T10-53 run (+519%, values `70,66,69,519,533,442,815`). **Verdict: not void, but cross-run absolute comparison against the 2026-08-22 baseline has only ~17% resolution.** Within-run comparisons are unaffected — and every conclusion in §7 rests on within-run only. **§7 SCATTER M3a — a real regression on OUR code, and a correction to this log's own history.** Two separate things had been conflated. **(a) The `tanstack/scatter n=1000` M3a series is NOT our code** — BENCHMARKS.md carries it as baseline `34.3` → observed `65.5` → `37.2` in the void run. This run measures it at **24.9 (−27.4% vs baseline)**. The `65.5` spike has now failed to reproduce **three consecutive times**; that specific watch is **CLOSED**, not merely downgraded. **(b) The regression is on `migrated`, and it is live.** Measured exclusively (`2026-08-24T21-01-31-188Z`) and compared *within session* against the same-run control: `scatter n=1000` — **bklit M1a 128.2 · M1b 1274.9 · M3a 32.6**; **tanstack (ceiling) 97.3 · 151.4 · 24.9**; **migrated 102.1 · 1192.1 · M3a 67.5**. Our mount→paint **beats** legacy (102.1 vs 128.2) and settle matches it (1192 vs 1275 — both carry the intentional 1100 ms reveal the ceiling lacks), **but M3a is 2.07× bklit's (67.5 vs 32.6)** on the hover/update path. Note `67.5 ≈ 65.5`: the number this log recorded as a `tanstack` spike is almost certainly **migrated's** all along, mislabelled — which is why it "did not reproduce" on the tanstack channel while remaining stubbornly present on ours. **This is a genuine MAIN-GOAL-relevant regression** (interactivity parity), it is measured against the control in a single session so no environmental drift explains it, and it is exactly P4.2's blast radius (T-C2 focus strategy + T-C7 broadcast store both sit on scatter's hover path). **`migrated/scatter/1000` M3a = 67.5 is the binding "before" number for P4.2**, which is why P4.2's own bench mandate was cancelled in favour of this lead-run exclusive cell. |

| **D287** | 2026-08-24 | **`kill <dispatch-pid>` DOES NOT KILL THE EXECUTOR — a zombie P3.9 `cmd` child wrote `bar-chart.tsx` for ~2.75h after I believed the run dead, invalidating one bench run and breaking the build. Binding procedure change below.** | **§1 The trap.** `dispatch.sh` is a *wrapper*: it spawns a child `node ~/.npm-global/bin/cmd -p --output-format json --model stealth/ox-alpha … -n "<title>" --yolo`. `kill <wrapper-pid>` reaps only the wrapper. The child keeps its own file handles, keeps burning turns, and **keeps writing to the working tree** — with no Monitor attached (the events stream dies with the wrapper), so it is completely invisible. When I killed 6672 at the 90-minute mark (D285 §6), child **6706** survived and ran on. **§2 How it surfaced — and the only reason it did.** Not by any gate. A harness *file-changed-on-disk* note said `bar-chart.tsx` had changed since I last read it, showing native-tooltip code I had never written and had already proven absent (`grep -c nativeTooltipExtension` → exactly `1`, the import, before my deletion). `ps aux \| grep npm-global/bin/cmd` then showed 6706, started 4:50PM. **Without that passive note the corruption was undetectable** — see §4. **§3 Three consequences, all real.** (a) `bar-chart.tsx` was left *non-compiling*: the zombie added `NATIVE_TOOLTIP_OFFSET`, `NativeTooltipBoxState`, `buildNativeTooltipOptions` and a local `ChartTooltipOptionsNative` type (65 lines) plus an `intFmt` import, all referencing the two `@tanstack/charts/tooltip` imports I had *just deleted* as D286's build fix — so my green D286 gate and the zombie's writes interleaved into a broken file. (b) The D286 gate (tsc 0 / build 0 / 10 charts PASS) no longer described the tree it certified. (c) Bench run `buct8rovs` had been running **concurrently with a live executor**, violating D273 exclusivity → discarded unmeasured. **§4 The generalised danger.** The zombie's code was *dead* (`buildNativeTooltipOptions` never called), so **all ten charts still passed on pixels** — QA cannot see this class of damage at all. Only `tsc` caught it, and only because the dead code referenced deleted symbols. **A zombie writing self-consistent dead code would have passed every gate in this project.** This is the same shape as D286 §0: the gate is green, the tree is wrong. **§5 BINDING PROCEDURE (do this every time).** (i) To kill a dispatch: `pkill -f "cmd .*-n <title>"` **or** kill the wrapper *and then* `ps aux \| grep npm-global/bin/cmd` and kill each surviving child by pid. (ii) **After every kill, assert the absence**: `ps aux \| grep -E "npm-global/bin/cmd\|dispatch.sh" \| grep -v grep` must print **nothing** before you trust any subsequent gate or start any bench. (iii) Same assertion before every bench run — D273 exclusivity is now something I *verify*, not assume. (iv) Extend D286's rule: after an interrupted dispatch, `git diff HEAD --stat -- <files in scope>` — and re-run it *after* the kill, since the window kept widening while I worked. **§6 Repair performed.** Excised the T-D8 block by *anchor* (not line number — `sed`/Read disagreed by one line, itself a symptom of a live writer), plus the dead `intFmt` import; 2401 chars / 67 lines removed. Asserted P3.10's accepted work survived (`nativeDepthGradients` at `:427`, `gradients:` at `:709`, dep at `:741`). `TSC_EXIT=0`, `BUILD_EXIT=0`, 29 routes. Re-ran the two charts whose pixels were captured mid-write: **`bar` reproduced the pre-repair numbers byte-for-byte** (0.0000 / 0.0000 / 0.0757 / 0.1243) — the D265 §5 identical-history check, and positive proof the zombie's code never painted; `bardepth` matched on all four shared phases with only `depth-off` 0.0756→0.0697 and `depth-on` 0.0181→0.0264 moving, both far inside threshold. **§7 Cost.** One invalid bench, one false-green gate, one build break, ~2.75h of unsupervised writes to a file under active review — from a single `kill` that reported success.

That is squarely MAIN GOAL clause 1, "exact same frontend design and interactivity." Native `ChartTooltipOptions` (`types.ts:1429-1457`) has **no equivalent for any of them**: `content()` must return plain `ChartTooltipContent` data painted by `paintStructuredTooltip` (`tooltip.ts:669-709`), never React. The React tooltip-**body** portal that would fix this exists — `react-charts/src/tooltip.tsx:85` wraps with `useTooltipBody` + `createPortal` — but **`index.ts` does not export it**, and `onTooltipBodyChange` lives only on `ChartImplementationProps` (`Chart.tsx:56-64`), stripped from public `ChartProps` (`:48-54`). Reaching into the clone for it is a D232 violation by construction. Today's implementation really does render React into the box (`tooltip-chrome.ts:450,453-454,487-488`, `createRoot`), so this is live capability, not theoretical. **Violates MAIN GOAL clause 3, "seamless change from Legacy Bklit" — a prop that compiles and does nothing is worse than a prop that fails.** **(3) BLOCKER C (minor, would have been tunable alone) — edge clamp differs: bklit `positionBox:385` clamps at `BOX_OFFSET` 16px, native hardcodes an 8px inset (`tooltip-placement.ts:34,37-40,84-88`).** Noted because it is the one gap that is *not* structural; it is disqualifying only alongside A and B. **(4) The horizontal-flip finding is worth keeping even though the package closed** — native `placement:['right','left']` is **algebraically identical** to bklit's flip (`tooltip-placement.ts:111-115` right=`x+gap`/left=`x−w−gap` vs `positionBox:383-384`), with `offset:16` = `BOX_OFFSET`. Dispatching the five remaining sub-runs would have rediscovered the identical two blockers five times. **Scope-cutting the dispatch to bar-as-pathfinder is what made one run answer six.** `internal/tooltip-chrome.ts` (624 lines) and `internal/tooltip-scheduler.ts` (67 lines) both **stay** — no un-wiring, no orphans, nothing to delete. **(6) Process note: this report exists only because the run was interrupted at the 90-minute mark and resumed report-only on the same session id.** The original dispatch was 90 min in, still investigating, zero edits — the exact shape of the five prior D277 zero-report runs. Killing the pid and re-dispatching with `--session 415648c0` under a "write your report now, an honest could-not-determine is fine" mandate preserved all the mapping context and produced the gap table above in ~4 minutes. **A pre-committed elapsed-time threshold plus a session-preserving resume converts the zero-report failure mode into a deliverable. Reuse it.** (First retry was killed externally at 4m08s with an empty output file — likely memory pressure from the concurrent P3.10 dispatch; a straight re-dispatch on the same session succeeded.) |

Independently corroborated: `reconcile.ts:11-32`'s `interpolatedAttributes` includes `opacity` but not `filter`. **(2) Per-mark durations are silently collapsed to a single `Math.max` in the renderer this app actually ships.** `ChartMarkState.transition` (`types.ts:218-229`) is per-definition at the *type* level, so 400/120/150 ms are all nominally expressible — but `resolveMarkStateScene` (`mark-state.ts:28-101`) accumulates every matching definition in one pass through `mergeTransition` (`:292-306`), whose tween branch is verbatim `duration: Math.max(current.duration ?? 250, next.duration ?? 250)`. A per-`markId` map (`ResolvedMarkState.transitions`, `:22-26`) does exist — but is consumed **only** by `createSvgMotionRuntime` (`motion.ts:893,911-912,922`), which this codebase does not use for focus. The shipped path, `@tanstack/react-charts` → `createSvgChartRenderer` → `svg-surface.ts:118-128`, passes only the single merged `state.transition ?? previousTransition`. **So composed's bar 0.12s + line/area 0.4s in one hover event would retime the bar UP to 400 ms.** This kills C11's per-series split at the mechanism level — the very thing D265 §3 accepted as landed. **(3) THE DECISIVE ONE, which no prior analysis (mine included) had noticed: hover dimming has TWO triggers, and only one of them is native focus.** Every dim is driven by native pointer/keyboard focus **and** by **legend hover**, which is a plain React **context** (`internal/chart-legend-hover.tsx:3-11`, `createContext`/`useContext`) living **entirely outside the SVG pointer system**. `hover-chrome.ts:273` calls `syncDim()` on legend change, and the legend branches are **interleaved with pointer-hover checks across 13 sites** (`:348, 371, 421, 426, 501, 529, 537, 550, 632, 641, 695`) inside the same code path. Native `states`/`paintFocus` resolves **only** against TanStack's `ChartFocusState`, and `ChartProps` exposes **no controlled-`focus` input** — only output callbacks (`Chart.tsx:35-38`). **This applies to every mark in every chart, so no chart is single-trigger enough to isolate even a pointer-only partial swap** without dropping legend-hover parity. Heatmap has the identical coupling (`heatmap-hover-chrome.ts:2-5`). **(4) Native's JS tween easing is not bklit's curve.** `reconcile.ts:334-341` implements `'ease-in-out'` as a **quadratic** (`t<0.5 ? 2t² : 1-(-2t+2)²/2`); bklit/migrated share the literal CSS `cubic-bezier(0.42,0,0.58,1)`. Since the baseline has bar at **0/0/0/0** and line at **0/0/0**, a curve substitution risks moving currently-byte-exact captures off zero **even with duration matched** — independent of blockers 1–3. **RULING: T-D11 is closed NON-VIABLE-AS-CHARTERED and removed from Wave 3. No re-dispatch.** Blockers 1 and 3 are not engineering-effort problems — they are "the native API does not model this," and 3 in particular means the swap cannot even be scoped down to a safe subset. **The custom hover-chrome engine STAYS, and this is now the documented reason it exists** — previously it was retained by default rather than by argument. **C11's per-series `dimOpacity` (D265 §3) is unaffected and remains correct** precisely *because* it lives on the custom mechanism; blocker 2 shows the native path would have broken it. **One legitimate future bridge, recorded but NOT chartered:** `ChartInteractionController.setControlledFocus(target, {source, pinned})` (`dom-types.ts:66-88`, impl `renderer.ts:487-528`), reachable via `context.interaction` in `onRender`, *is* architecturally capable of driving native focus from legend hover — but its `pinned` option also gates `tooltipIsSticky()`/`cursorBinding()?.pin` (`renderer.ts:519-521`), so a naive call would show/pin a tooltip on legend hover, which never happens today. That is a **focus-model migration**, not a dim-mechanism swap — new scope with its own regression surface. **Flagged to the user's `PLAN-phase-5.md`, which already proposes re-platforming interaction/focus onto sanctioned APIs — this is precisely that territory and belongs there, not in Phase 4.** **METHOD NOTE (§5), the counterpart to D265 §0: when a lead pre-settles viability to save an executor time, that ruling must state what evidence would OVERTURN it.** I wrote "do not re-litigate it" with no escape hatch; had the executor obeyed literally, it would have force-fit a swap into a type system that cannot hold it, and the pixel gate would have caught only blocker 4. Pre-settling is still correct — it prevents rediscovery churn — but every such ruling now carries an explicit falsifier. **Non-viability findings are a first-class deliverable, and this one is worth more than the swap would have been:** it converts four "we should probably do this someday" assumptions into settled, cited negatives. |
| **D268** | 2026-08-24 | **Read-only investigation of three blocking questions. Result: T-D10's acceptance criterion is far more broken than I flagged (it covers 2 of ~11 spring configs); the `Background` collision is genuinely harmless; and the "one `export default` per file" blocker is real but I had it stated BACKWARDS — the constraint is not where I said it was. Two new findings, one of them a duplicate constant nobody has counted.** **(1) T-D10 (P3.12) — the tolerance-pair criterion covers 2 of ~11 distinct spring configs, and the two it names are not even the contested ones.** I had flagged that `{300,30}`/`{180,28}` fail to cover ring/pie's `(400,25)`. Confirmed, and worse: **those two pairs are literally `TOOLTIP_SPRING` (`design-tokens.ts:29`) and `HIGHLIGHT_SPRING` (`design-tokens.ts:31`) copied verbatim** — the criterion names two existing tokens and silently omits every other spring in the tree. Full enumeration found **~11 distinct fixed configs plus 2 parametric families**: `TOOLTIP_BOX_SPRING` `{100,20}` (dynamically remapped over stiffness ≈[12–500], `chart-config-context.tsx:63-100`), `ENTRANCE_SPRING` `{300,25}`, `TICK_SPRING` `{180,24}` (`live-hover-chrome.ts:25`), `LABEL_DIM_SPRING` `{300,24}` (`funnel-hover-chrome.ts:41`), a **per-ring funnel family** `{300−i·60, 24−i·3}` (`funnel-geometry.ts:97-99`, one independent spring per ring layer), an **unnamed inline `{400,35}`** (`tooltip-chrome.ts:537-538`), an **unnamed inline `{300,25}`** at a distinct site (`heatmap-components.tsx:848`), radar's label spring `{80,15,mass:1}` (`radar-chart.tsx:504,633`), `GAUGE_SPRING_FALLBACK` `{300,20,mass:1}`, candlestick's duration/bounce-derived spring (`candle-spring.ts:68-135`), and `enter-transition.ts:71-79`'s `springFromBounce`. Also confirmed **dead/aspirational**: `sankey-animation.ts:31-39` declares `stiffness`/`damping`/`bounce` fields that `resolveTiming` (`:44-54`) never branches on — it always resolves to a tween, so that field set is not a real spring config and must not be counted as one. **(2) Ring/pie's `(400,25)` is confirmed verbatim — and is an UNCOUNTED DUPLICATE.** `ring-hover-chrome.ts:53` and `pie-hover-chrome.ts:37` **each independently declare** `const HOVER_SPRING = { stiffness: 400, damping: 25 } as const`, both citing legacy verbatim. That is the same layer-contract violation as D264 §2's four reveal-constant copies — a shared value re-inlined per call site, invisible to the synthesis because each copy is locally consistent. **No T-C row names it. Routed to P4.4** alongside D264 §2's copies. **(3) Native `createChartSpring` is a numeric SUPERSET, so the swap is not blocked on expressiveness — it is blocked on two things the criterion never mentions.** Signature `createChartSpring(input: ChartSpringOptions = {})` accepting arbitrary `stiffness`/`damping`/`mass`/`restSpeed`/`restDelta` (defaults `170/26/1/0.01/0.005`), with **no** bounce/duration shorthand at all — so `{400,25}`, `{80,15,1}`, `{300,20,1}`, `{400,35}` can all be passed directly. The real obstacles: **(a) architecture inversion.** Native is a **stateless analytic sampler** — `sample(elapsedMs, {from,to,velocity})`, closed-form damped-harmonic-oscillator — where **the caller owns the rAF loop and must persist retarget state itself**. The migrated `internal/spring.ts:38` `createSpring` is the exact opposite: a **stateful, self-driving object** (`set`/`jump`/`stop`) that owns its rAF loop internally. This is not a drop-in swap; it needs an adapter wrapper at **13+ call sites** (`ring-hover-chrome.ts:108`, `pie-hover-chrome.ts:147,151,168`, `hover-chrome.ts:221-222`, 7+ in `tooltip-chrome.ts`, `candlestick-hover-chrome.ts:143-144`, `live-hover-chrome.ts:161,329`, `funnel-hover-chrome.ts:79,87`, `heatmap-components.tsx:848`). **(b) different numerical method.** Migrated integrates **semi-implicit Euler at 120 Hz substeps** (`spring.ts:52-70`) with **two adaptive rest-threshold tiers** chosen per-retarget by delta magnitude (GRANULAR restDelta 0.005/restSpeed 0.01 vs DEFAULT 0.5/2, `:23-27,82-84`); native is closed-form with **fixed** thresholds. For identical `{stiffness,damping,mass}` the two produce visually indistinguishable but **not bit-identical** curves. **(4) RULING on T-D10 — re-scoped, and the acceptance criterion is replaced.** The criterion "spring motion within visual tolerance" gated by two named pairs cannot validate this swap, because the pixel gate samples hover at a **fixed 700 ms** wait: a curve that differs slightly mid-flight lands at a different position at capture time, producing diffs that are real but not regressions. **That is precisely ring's D258 pathology** (never settles → the 700 ms capture samples a random phase), so applying an Euler→analytic change to ring's hover spring risks manufacturing exactly the nondeterminism P4.3 is chartered to remove. Therefore: **(i)** T-D10 is scoped to the **already-closed-form, WAAPI-sampled** springs — candlestick (`candle-spring.ts`'s `createSpringResolver` is *already* the same closed-form branch structure as native's), gauge's fallback, radar's label spring, and `enter-transition.ts`'s spring path — where native's math is the same family and exactness is achievable if `restDelta`/`restSpeed` are matched explicitly; **(ii)** the **rAF-driven hover springs are EXCLUDED** unless the adapter is built and proven separately, and ring's is excluded outright until **P4.3 lands its oscillation fix** — swapping the integrator under an unsettled loop makes both changes unattributable; **(iii)** the acceptance criterion becomes **per-config**: name the actual configs swapped and assert the resolved `{stiffness,damping,mass,restDelta,restSpeed}` match, rather than asserting membership in two arbitrary pairs. Hover captures remain mandatory for ring per D255/D256/D258. **(5) The "one `export default` per file" blocker — REAL, but I had it backwards, and the fix is cheap.** I recorded it as a constraint hitting ring/radar. Direct check: **`ring-chart.tsx` and `radar-chart.tsx` contain ZERO `export default` statements** — both are named-exports-only, so no uniqueness constraint is being hit there at all, and the migrated public surface already matches legacy's barrel symbol-for-symbol via named re-exports (`index.ts:24-45` radar, `:62-70` ring). In *that* framing it is a phantom. **But the actual constraint is elsewhere and it is genuine:** legacy splits each family across **one file per component, each with exactly one `export default`** (radar: `radar-chart.tsx:262`, `radar-labels.tsx:80`, `radar-grid.tsx:128`, `radar-context.tsx:174`, `radar-area.tsx:225`, `radar-axis.tsx:58`; ring: `ring-chart.tsx:491`, `ring-context.tsx:188`, `ring.tsx:235`, `ring-center.tsx:119`) — and **legacy's `package.json` carries a WILDCARD subpath export, `"./*": "./src/*.tsx"`**, which makes every one of those defaults publicly reachable as `import RadarGrid from "@bklitui/ui/charts/radar-grid"`. So T-E2's premise holds (and go-to-plan `:181` already had this right: *"0 default exports on either barrel… the ~10 missing default exports in parity rows are part-file level — T-E2 unaffected"*). The real constraint is that **migrated consolidated 6 legacy radar modules into 1 file, and 6 default exports cannot coexist in one file.** **Ruling: satisfy T-E2 with thin default-re-export SHIM MODULES at legacy's file paths** (`radar-grid.ts` → `export { RadarGrid as default } from "./radar-chart"`), **not by splitting the consolidated files.** Shims are near-zero-risk, cost no pixels, and T-E1a's Files column already anticipates "shim modules." **P5.4 must first verify whether migrated ships an equivalent wildcard subpath map** — if it does not, the deep-import surface is unreachable regardless and T-E2 shrinks accordingly; that check gates the package's real size. **(6) RD2 "radar ×6" is 5 actionable — CONFIRMED with evidence.** The six legacy radar modules are radar-chart / radar-labels / radar-grid / radar-area / radar-axis / **radar-context**. The first five are components with migrated equivalents (`RadarChart`/`RadarLabels`/`RadarGrid`/`RadarArea`/`RadarAxis`); the sixth is `RadarStableContext`, a **context provider**, which DOC-4 routes to **ACCEPT**, not restore. So the "×6" label overstates the work by one and my earlier flag was right. **(7) NEW real gap, already covered — legacy's hook/context/token surface is entirely unexported by migrated.** Legacy's public barrel exports `RadarProvider`, `useRadar`, `useRadarHover`, `useRadarStable`, `radarCssVars`, `defaultRadarColors`, `RadarContextValue` (`repos/bklit-ui/packages/ui/src/charts/index.ts:454-464`) and the Ring equivalents (`:481-489`). **Migrated re-exports none of them.** Sharpest detail: **`useRingStable` and `useRingHoverCoordinator` already EXIST** at `ring-chart.tsx:119,129` — they are defined and simply never barrel-exported, making that portion a one-line fix rather than a port. This is already assigned (T-E1b names `useRing*` R2–R4 and `useRadar*` explicitly), so **no new task** — but it is a genuine "identical public component API" gap and P5.2 should treat the already-defined hooks as the cheap half. **(8) The `Background` collision is COSMETIC — flag retired.** Two symbols, both real: the public **role-marker carrier** `children.tsx:133-137` (renders `null`, tagged `CHART_ROLE = "background"`, consumed by `extractChildren` at `:328`; this is the one exported at `index.ts:214`) and the actual **renderer** `internal/background.tsx:27` (my note said `:26` — off by one). No harm, verified rather than assumed: the renderer is imported **exactly once**, by `internal/background-layer.tsx:9`, which immediately re-exports it as `BackgroundLayer`, and **every chart file imports only `BackgroundLayer`** (`area:41`, `bar:51`, `scatter:38`, `candlestick:41`, `composed:100`, `line:23`). **No file imports both symbols, so no shadowing, no ambiguous resolution, no public-API collision.** The carrier/renderer split is not an oddity either — it is applied **uniformly to every compositional child** in `children.tsx`, because `defineChart` must extract a config from JSX *before* rendering, unlike legacy's live-tree render. Optional clarity-only improvement: rename the internal export to `BackgroundRenderer`. Not a defect; no task. |

No further specification needed; **the flag was mine and it was wrong.** Recording it rather than silently dropping it, since the same flag would otherwise be re-raised at P5.3 dispatch. **(3) Method note for the remaining flag backlog.** Both items here, plus D266's FD6 finding, share one cause: **a task label survives in the plan after the work moved or landed elsewhere.** Three of my open flags have now resolved to "already done or already assigned, plan not updated" and only one (D266 §2's P5.3/P6.3 split) to a genuine conflict needing a decision. **So the default posture when clearing a flag should be verify-current-state-first, not design-a-resolution-first** — the cheap grep retires more flags than the careful ruling does, and a ruling written over already-landed work is worse than no ruling because it schedules a re-implementation. |

(b) The P5.3/P6.3 double-claim on the same D-numbers is real, but the correct fix is to split by *content*, not to award the D-number to one of them.** **(1) FD6 (`resolveLineLoadingPulseMode`) — NOT a gap; assignment found. Flag retired.** `DOC-10` (`go-to-plan.md:19`) instructs *"export `resolveLineLoadingPulseMode` (FD6)"*, and I had flagged that T-E5 (`:160`) — the loading-presets task DOC-10 otherwise feeds — omits it, leaving FD6 apparently unassigned. It is assigned: **T-E1a (`:154`) names `resolveLineLoadingPulseMode` explicitly** in its re-export worksheet, among the surviving-backing-module symbols. The confusion is a doctrine-vs-mechanism split that the docs never spell out: DOC-10 rules on the loading family's *substance* (what stays, what's accepted-inert, what gets wired), while the *mechanism* for FD6 specifically is a **barrel re-export**, which is T-E1a's whole remit. **Ruling: FD6 executes in P5.1 (T-E1a), not P5.5 (T-E5); T-E5 needs no FD6 line and its omission is correct, not an oversight.** Worth recording because the natural reflex — "DOC-10 mentions it, so add it to T-E5" — would have produced a **duplicate export** in two packages, i.e. the flag itself was the more dangerous artifact than the thing it flagged. **(2) The P5.3 (T-E1c) vs P6.3 (T-F2) collision is real and confirmed by both rows' own Files column — both are `docs/phase-4/LOG.md`.** T-E1c (`:156`) writes a *"Barrel ACCEPT ledger"* citing **D240** (DOC-4) and **D244** (DOC-10), requiring *"every ACCEPTed symbol named explicitly in the D-log entry."* T-F2 (`:170`) writes *"D-log entries: **D240** (provider architecture), D241 (event model), D242 (enterTransition reshape), D243 (bar pilot scope), **D244** (loading package), D245 (radar label spring)… + every ACCEPT row from parity.md not already D-ruled."* So D240 and D244 are each claimed by two packages a full wave apart. **Ruling: split by content, and do NOT apply D263 §3's "earlier package owns the file" doctrine here.** That doctrine settles *file* overlaps, where two packages would edit the same lines; this is not that — the two packages have **genuinely different content** that the go-to-plan merely happened to file under one number each. P5.3's deliverable is a **symbol ledger** (an enumeration of accepted barrel symbols); P6.3's is an **architectural ruling** (why the provider/loading/event design is what it is). Collapsing either into the other loses information, and awarding the number to the earlier package would force P6.3 to append architecture to a symbol list. Therefore: **(a)** P5.3 writes its own standalone entry containing the barrel ACCEPT ledger and must **not** label itself D240/D244; **(b)** P6.3 writes separate entries for the architectural rulings and **cross-references P5.3's ledger entry rather than re-listing symbols** — the ledger is the enumeration of record; **(c)** neither package uses the suggested numbers **D240–D245 at all**, per D263 §7: D240 already exists as an unrelated Wave-0 bench entry and D241–D245 are genuinely absent, so both take next-available numbers and cite the stale suggested number inline for traceability. **(3) Incidental — one pending cleanup item closed itself.** `f4-repo-root-scratch/` no longer exists in the repo root; nothing to delete. Also noted while checking: **`PLAN-phase-5.md` (2026-08-23) and a `research/phase-5/` corpus exist** as pre-existing user-authored work, not agent output. Its "P5.x" identifiers are a **different namespace** from this plan's wave-5 package labels (`P5.1`…`P5.8`), which is a live collision risk for anyone reading both — the funnel routing in D264 §4 goes to **Phase 4's wave-5 package P5.7**, not to anything in PLAN-phase-5.md. Flagged for the user rather than acted on: PLAN-phase-5 proposes re-platforming interaction/focus and tooltips onto sanctioned APIs, which is the same territory as Phase 4's T-D11 and T-D8, so some Phase 4 native-swap work may be superseded by it. It was visible in the very first run **only because excused errors are still counted and printed** — the rule I wrote in as "a silent allowlist is how a real regression hides behind an accepted one." Had the gate swallowed matches quietly, a dead pattern would have been indistinguishable from a working one, and the entry would have read as protecting a class of error it was in fact ignoring. Pattern corrected to `/attribute viewBox: A negative value is not valid/` and hoisted to a shared `NEGATIVE_VIEWBOX` const; the failure mode is documented in-file so the next author does not re-derive it. Re-run confirms both funnels now `PASS — 1000 error(s) EXCUSED` with the sample printed. |

| **D255** | 2026-08-23 | **Moving-hit-area audit CLOSED. Exactly one AFFECTED site remains repo-wide (ring) and it is ruled ACCEPT, not FIX, because legacy has the identical defect. The audit's most useful output is the negative result.** Dispatched after pie (D254) and sunburst (D251) turned out to be two instances of one root pattern — *a hover effect that moves or grows the element carrying the hover listener*. Read-only, worked from the mechanism rather than a chart list, and explicitly guarded against over-reporting. **(1) The negative result is the headline: the pattern is not systemic.** Every other hover site is safe, and for identifiable structural reasons worth recording, because they are the shapes that make a port safe: **funnel** binds to a static label-overlay div entirely separate from the animated svg (matching legacy); **heatmap** resolves hover by `pointermove` **coordinate hit-testing** against a static container — positional math is immune to cell movement, and is strictly *safer* than legacy, which scales per-cell wrapper `<g>`s with cell-level hover; **bar/composed/stacked** and **candlestick** route through the TanStack focus system over static column-track bands and draw highlight chrome in a separate overlay layer, never on data rects; **choropleth** and **sankey** change opacity only, never geometry. Two are safe by geometry rather than structure: **radar areas** scale 1.05 about an interior origin, so a star polygon only grows outward and cannot eject an interior cursor; the **marker fan** opens inside a static superset box whose enter/leave batch within one task, leaving net state unchanged. **(2) Ring — the one AFFECTED site, ruled ACCEPT.** `ring-chart.tsx:725` binds `pointerenter` to the same track group that `ring-hover-chrome.ts:104` scales by 1.03. Scaling an *annulus* about its center makes the **inner** edge recede outward by ~`0.03 × innerRadius` (≈3–4px), and unlike pie there is no sibling surface behind the hole to catch the cursor — a pointer parked in that thin inner band can drop out and oscillate. **Ruled ACCEPT because legacy bklit is structurally identical** (`repos/bklit-ui/.../ring.tsx:179-188` binds `onMouseEnter/Leave` to the same `motion.g` it springs): this is an inherited latent defect, not migration drift, and hardening it would be a deviation under **DOC-1** (match legacy), which is the whole point of the migration. Ring currently passes its gates, which is consistent with the ejection band being thin enough that the probes never park in it. **Not fixed, but not forgotten** — a cross-ref is recorded in go-to-plan.md's ordering table against **T-D10 (P3.12)** and **T-C4 (P4.3)**, because the defect is spring-driven and geometry-coupled and P3.12 swaps the spring integrator for `createChartSpring`; any change to overshoot or settle timing changes how long the inner edge sits displaced. That cross-ref carries two things: P3.12's ring gate **must include hover captures** (an oscillation is invisible to a settled diff), and the diagnostic to run first if ring ever fails (hover just inside the inner rim, count `pointerenter`/`pointerleave` churn over ~2s) plus the parity-neutral hardening if it is ever needed (bind listeners to an **unscaled wrapper `<g>`** around track+progress — zero pixel change). **(3) Radar dots — safe today, but by an undocumented invariant**, recorded so it is not lost. A dot's hover scale translates it radially and *can* eject a cursor at its trailing edge; it is harmless only because the ejected cursor lands inside the same-series area path, which scales by the same factor about the same origin and sets the *same* index, so state holds. That safety silently depends on the area path existing, being painted, and listening — a future points-without-areas variant turns dots into lone moving listeners and the case becomes AFFECTED. No code change; one comment when radar is next touched. **(4) The closing observation, which reframes the two earlier bugs.** Legacy bklit builds an explicit static hit target in exactly **two** places — sunburst's hit path and pie's hitbox pair — and those are **precisely** the two charts where the migration dropped the protection and later had to restore it. Everywhere else legacy itself binds to the moving element. So this was never a systemic porting habit: it was two specific defenses that legacy had bothered to build and the port did not notice were load-bearing. That is a sharper lesson than "audit all hover code" — **where legacy did something structurally odd, assume it was defending against something.** **(5) Left open, honestly reported by the audit rather than guessed:** whether ring's oscillation actually manifests (needs the empirical trace above); scatter's classification rests on the *absence* of mark-level listeners in a global grep rather than a full read of `scatter-hover-chrome.ts`; and bar/scatter focus routing was inferred from the focus-strategy files rather than asserted from a DOM dump. None blocks Wave 2; all three are cheap to close if a related gate ever fails. |

| **D251** | 2026-08-23 | **Fix dispatch F1 (sunburst) CLOSED — P1.7 ticked; all three Wave 1 gate findings now resolved. Two defects fixed, and the root cause is a TanStack-reconciler interaction worth remembering across the rest of Phase 4.** Lead-verified against disk (`overallPass=true` on both runs; note F1 also added an **untracked new file**, `internal/sunburst-hit.tsx`, which a `git diff --stat` alone would have missed — `git status` is the check that catches this). **(1) The Wave 1 regression — root cause is TanStack's SVG reconciler, not our code.** `showcase/repos/tanstack-charts/packages/charts-core/src/reconcile.ts` `syncAttributes` (L146–148) strips **every** attribute on the live stage svg that the fresh markup doesn't carry, and no TanStack markup ever emits `data-bkm-*`. Every hover re-render therefore flows: pointerenter → hover-grow baked into `arcRows` → new `definition` → `adapter.update()` sees `definitionChanged` → full reconcile → **`data-bkm-revealed` and `data-bkm-reveal-cycle` wiped** → `onRender` → `handleRender`. Pre-Wave-1 this wipe was **harmless**, because the in-memory `seenRevealedRef` still held every arc index so `toReveal` came out empty. P1.7's new dataset-compare block (`svgForBkm.dataset.bkmRevealCycle !== playCycleRef.current`) defeated that guard: right after a wipe the comparison is *always* true → `seen.clear()` → every arc counts as unrevealed → **full 1100ms staggered reveal replays on every hover**. QA's `HOVER_WAIT_MS` is 700ms, so captures land mid-replay. This accounts for every number D248 recorded: `settled` byte-identical (no hover → no reconcile → stamps intact), hover captures diverging capture-dependently, and worse at n=33 (more arcs → longer stagger). Fix: the dataset-compare block is deleted and replaced with a comment documenting the trap; cycle identity now lives wholly in `playCycleRef` inside the SB1 replay effect. **P1.7's actual goal is preserved** — `playKey` replay still works, as required by the dispatch's explicit instruction not to trade one defect for another — along with SB12 label cull, SB13 center-radius interpolate, SB14 commit-immediately zoom, and SB15 fade + svg pointerleave. **Generalized lesson: any `data-*` stamp written on the stage svg is erased by the next reconcile.** DOM-stamp state is not durable anywhere in this codebase; ref-held state is. This matters beyond sunburst — it should be checked wherever a part stamps the stage, and especially during the W3 native swaps, which increase reconcile frequency. **(2) A second, PRE-EXISTING defect found while gating.** After fix 1, hover-30 still failed at 0.7701% — better than the 0.9478% pre-Wave-1 baseline, so the residual predates Wave 1 and was never P1.7's doing. Event-trace probes showed the hover-30 probe sits **exactly on the Branch1/Branch5 shared diameter**. bklit hit-tests transparent fill-only paths at **base** geometry (`sunburst-segment.tsx`: hit path plus a `pointerEvents:"none"` visual path, no per-segment leave) and *misses* that knife-edge point, so last-enter-wins keeps Leaf 1.1. Migrated's listeners sat on TanStack-rendered paths carrying **grown** geometry plus a 1px stroke → deterministic hit on Branch 1 → different dim/grow chrome → 0.77%. Fix: new `internal/sunburst-hit.tsx` ports bklit's hit model (transparent fill-only paths at base geometry, zoom-morphed during zoom; depth-descending DOM order so parents win at boundaries; no per-segment leave; svg-level leave clears). TanStack visual paths deliberately retain **click** listeners because the bench scenario's `__benchDrilldown` synthetic-dispatch contract clicks them directly — verified still working. **Gate:** sunburst files typecheck clean; n=27 `qa/results/sunburst/2026-08-23T14-28-35-953Z` settled 0.0599 / hover-30 **0.0780** / hover-50 **0.1400** / hover-70 **0.1466**, all PASS; n=1000 `…T14-20-54-053Z` 0.0599/0.0212/0.0199/0.0228, all PASS, matching the pre-Wave-1 n=1000 baseline; console sunburst 0 errors/0 warnings; drilldown functionally verified (hit-layer click, synthetic `__benchDrilldown`, center zoom-out, hover after zoom round-trip). **hover-30 at 0.078% is now the best capture on the chart — better than the pre-Wave-1 PASSing ~0.32%.** `tooltipVisibleA/B` remain false on all sunburst captures; that is the long-standing sunburst norm and was explicitly excluded from scope. **Cross-wave consequence already recorded** in go-to-plan.md's ordering table: the invariant *"reveal plays on mount and on `playKey` change, never on hover"* joins SB13/SB14 as behavior T-D12 (P3.6) must reproduce, and P3.6's gate must include **hover** captures at n=27/n=33 — a settled-only gate would have passed this entire regression, since `settled` never moved off 0.0599%. **Leftovers cleared by the lead:** F1's probe scripts (`scratch-f1/`, 5 files) plus a stray executor-created directory at a typo'd path **outside the repo** (`~/bklit-tanstack-chars-migration/` — "chars", not "charts", holding one probe file); both removed, probes preserved outside the repo as evidence. Executors cannot delete (guard policy), so scratch cleanup is standing lead work. **Flagged, not acted on:** F1 reported 4 `TS2561 'tooltipBoxSpring' does not exist… did you mean 'tooltipSpring'?` errors in `bar-chart.tsx:709`, `candlestick-chart.tsx:691`, `composed-chart.tsx:1108`, `internal/use-hover-chrome.ts:90` — these are **P2.2's in-flight K-1 work** (deleting the per-chrome `tooltipBoxSpring` option), mid-edit at the time. F1 correctly refused to patch files outside its fences. P2.2 must land the tree at tsc exit 0; this is the specific thing to verify when it reports. |
| **D250** | 2026-08-23 | **Fix dispatches F2 (choropleth) and F3 (heatmap) CLOSED — both T-W1-9 halves now meet "hover visuals indistinguishable from legacy"; P1.4 and P1.5 ticked. Both root causes were things D248's triage got wrong, and that is the useful part of this entry.** Every claim below was re-verified by the lead against the on-disk `report.json`, not accepted from the report. **(1) choropleth — root cause was NEITHER hypothesis the dispatch was given.** D248 offered (a) hit-testing rejecting the coordinate or (b) a missing legacy tooltip *flip* rendering out of view, and named (b) the leading candidate because flip is CP7's own charter. **Both were disproved.** (a): `isPointInFill` at the probe point (339.6, 319.875) hits **no path in either impl** — that point is open Atlantic, off every feature; geometry is identical (settled 0.0000%). (b): CP7's flip is correctly implemented (`positionBox` flips+clamps; tooltip layer has `pointer-events:none`) — the tooltip never fired at all, so nothing was rendered off-screen. **The actual defect is hover-lifecycle persistence.** The QA trajectory enters Canada → USA → rests over ocean. In **bklit**, hovering a feature makes React **unmount** the hovered `base-` path and mount a `highlight-` copy (`choropleth-feature.tsx` StaticFeatureLayer); when the pointer leaves into ocean **no `mouseleave` is ever delivered** because the entered element no longer exists, so USA's hover + tooltip **stick**. In **migrated**, the imperative chrome keeps the same path connected, its `mouseleave` fired, `clearHover()` ran → no tooltip, no dim. That is why 1.5345% never moved across P1.4's 217-line rewrite: **P1.4 never touched this semantic, and neither would any amount of flip work.** Fix confined to `internal/choropleth-hover-chrome.ts` (path `mouseleave` no longer clears by default; bklit's one clearing nuance — re-entering the hovered feature arms its leave, since bklit's highlight path *is* live — preserved via a `pathLeaveArmed` flag; svg-level `mouseleave`/`pointerleave` still clear). **No shared file touched**, so no other-chart re-gating was required. Verified with an 8-step identical-trajectory parity matrix (enter→ocean-stick, re-enter→leave-clear, A→B switch, svg-exit clear, stick-then-exit) — bklit and migrated match on every step. Gate: tsc exit 0; `qa/results/choropleth/2026-08-23T13-03-30-947Z` settled 0.0000%, hover-30 **0.0000%** (was 1.5345% FAIL, `tooltipVisibleB=false`), hover-50 0.1529%, hover-70 0.0071%, tooltips true/true everywhere, `overallPass=true`; independent self-test `2026-08-23T13-05-25-679Z` all four 0.0000%; console clean. **(2) heatmap — verdict (b), the residual was never HM16.** HM16 *was* genuinely removed by P1.5 (the 1.5px highlight-ring `<rect>` is gone from `heatmap-components.tsx`), and that accounted for the ~0.013pp D248 measured. The stubborn ~0.08pp overshoot was a **separate, pre-existing dim-mechanism divergence**: **legacy** (`repos/bklit-ui/.../heatmap-cells.tsx` ~L414) tweens the data rect's **own** `style.opacity` down to `inactiveOpacity` (0.3); **migrated** kept the cell at full opacity and painted a background-colored overlay rect (`fillOpacity = 1 − inactiveOpacity`) on top. Over a white ground the end states are mathematically identical **except at antialiased cell edges**, where the overlay's own edges composite differently — producing the hollow-square outlines scattered through the diff PNGs. Fix confined to `internal/heatmap-components.tsx`: `paintCellStyles` now tweens the cell rect's own opacity (same 0.22s `cubic-bezier(0.4,0,0.2,1)`), overlay dim rects + `hoverDimOpacity` memo deleted; the overlay `<svg>` stays because it hosts the pattern defs. **All T-W1-7 work preserved** (HM14 pattern fills, HM7 `useId` separator gradients, HM6 separator stroke, `heatmapLevelCellFillOpacity` composition). Gate: tsc exit 0; `qa/results/heatmap/2026-08-23T12-27-16-808Z` (**n=52**, the mandated density) settled 0.4277%, hover-30 **0.3466%**, hover-50 **0.3533%**, hover-70 **0.4256%**, tooltips true both sides, `overallPass=true` — hover dropped 0.16–0.17pp, closing an ~0.08pp overshoot **with margin rather than marginally**; console sweep PASS incl. the `state=loading` load. **Process notes.** Both dispatches were truncated repeatedly by the `stealth/ox-alpha` backend (`state="completed"` on a mid-thought body, zero file edits) — F2 four times, F3 three — and both were recovered by an auto-resume driver keyed on the report citing a real `qa/results/<chart>/` run id, since `state="completed"` carries no information. **Two mid-flight findings were hand-carried into the task files as lead notes** so they would survive a broken session chain; F3's note (the dim-mechanism hypothesis) proved correct **and the executor verified it against source rather than taking it on faith**, which is the behavior wanted. **Leftovers handled by the lead:** F2's probe scripts (`scratch-f1/cp30/`, untracked, 8 files) removed from the repo — executors cannot delete (guard policy) — with the 8-step parity harness preserved outside the repo as evidence. **Routed forward:** F3 reported `paintHeatmapCellHover` (`internal/heatmap-hover-chrome.ts:144`) as dead code from an earlier approach and correctly left it alone as out of scope → added to **P2.1**'s deletion list (the package holding heatmap write access), with an explicit instruction to re-prove deadness rather than inherit the claim. |

| **D247** | 2026-08-23 | **Wave 1 implementation closed: all 15 packages (P1.1–P1.15) landed, settled tree typechecks exit 0. Two deviations from D246 recorded.** **(a) Concurrency cap deviation — 15-wide, not D246's ~5–6 (user-directed: "Can we parallize more?").** D246's cap existed to protect QA/bench port contention and the exclusive bench slot; the D246 impl/gate split had *already* removed both from package-executor scope (packages implement + typecheck only). Cost paid: with 15 executors on one working tree, nearly every package reported non-zero `tsc` from *other* packages' mid-edit files — every executor correctly refused to patch files it did not own and reported honestly instead. Two genuine cross-cluster fixes were made by executors (P1.4 wired the dangling `laidOutNodesRef` into `createSankeyMark`; P1.10 fixed P1.13's `BackgroundConfig extends …PatternPresetOptions` TS2499 by aliasing it). **(b) Two harness fixtures authored by the lead, not an executor.** P1.1 and P1.3 both correctly refused to write into `bench/` (fenced) and flagged that their acceptance criteria — prop-less `<Grid />` default, and `stacked`+`stackGap` matching legacy — had **no pixel gate at all**. This cost two 30s-timeout QA failures misdiagnosed as a stale preview server before a Playwright `pageerror` probe surfaced the real throw. **Notable package findings:** P1.13 found the top-level `ticks:` key on axis specs was **dead across all six hosts** — vendored charts-core only reads `axis.ticks.count`, so grid row density never reached the grid and fell back to TanStack's auto `height/48`; rewired, which means grid-density diffs should SHRINK and any growth is a real finding. P1.7 found `playKey` was **inert** (`onRender` cannot refire on an unchanged chart definition); fixed with an imperative replay effect. P1.15 changed `fadeEdges` to any-non-false semantics; P1.13 limited `Background` enter-fade to line/area. **Tooling defect to fix later:** the executor guard hook pattern-matches the word "truncate" in file *content* as a deletion primitive and blocked legitimate file creation (reported by P1.12). **Provider stall, no data loss:** the gate-runner's `stealth/ox-alpha` stream hung for a single 474-minute gap mid-turn while holding an open TCP connection, then resumed on its own and completed at 18/150 turns — worth knowing that a silent multi-hour stall is a failure mode of this model backend, and that the session `.jsonl` (not the dispatch log, which stays empty until the end) is where liveness is actually visible. |
| **D240** | 2026-08-22 | **Wave-0 B4 (exclusive `bench --all` + sankey visual refs) CLOSED — fixed a real v0.14.0 bench-harness regression, not a load/timing issue.** First `bench --all` since `7d60a7f` found every `tanstack`-impl cell SKIPPED: `page.waitForFunction: Timeout 30000ms exceeded` on `window.__benchPaintDone`, because the scenario page crashed pre-paint with `TypeError: Cannot use 'in' operator to search for 'create' in true` — 10 `bench/app/src/scenarios/tanstack-*.tsx` files passed the bare boolean `tooltip: true` into `defineChart`, but v0.14's `resolveTooltipInput()` (charts-core `renderer.ts:1142`) requires `false \| ChartTooltipExtension \| options`, not a boolean, and throws on `'create' in true`. QA never caught this because `qa/screenshot.mjs` compare runs had been serving an older, pre-regression dist (`QA_SKIP_REBUILD=1`); bench forced a fresh rebuild and hit the broken bundle. `showcase/migrated/` itself was unaffected — its internal host already consumes `{enabled: true}` before TanStack sees it; only the bench app's own scenario harness passed the raw boolean through. Fix (bench-app-confined, 10 files): `import { tooltip } from "@tanstack/charts/tooltip"` (the sanctioned DOM extension token) + `tooltip: true,` → `tooltip,`. Zero clone edits (D232), zero showcase edits, zero bench timing/data changes — confirmed via post-fix live probes (no pageerrors) and 8/8 single-cell verification (tanstack M1a 24–53ms, M3a ~32ms vs bklit M3a ~31ms, sane order of magnitude, `tooltipAppeared=true` everywhere). Full exclusive rerun `bench/results/2026-08-22T15-46-19-823Z`: **24/24 cells, 0 skipped** (line/area/bar/scatter × bklit/tanstack × n=100/1000/10000); both impls degrade together on the known-degenerate bar n=10000 class; tanstack stays sub-second on M1b everywhere (no reveal by design) vs bklit's intentional ~1.1s reveal floor. One anomaly named, not fixed (pre-existing, unrelated to this patch): `bklit/line n=1000` reports `tooltipAppeared=false` in a hover observation — bklit-side, flagged for later, not a bench-harness or v0.14 issue. Sankey visual refs (B4's other half) verified against the three post-D239 QA dirs (n=4/33/1000) — no re-capture needed. `docs/phase-4/BENCHMARKS.md` closed out (§4.4.1 baseline run id + full headline table, B2 sankey row, Wave-0 gate table P0.1b + P0.2 bench cells). Temp scratch scripts (`scripts/b4b-probe.mjs`, `scripts/sankey-console-check.mjs`) deleted. **Wave-0/P0.2 baseline (4.4.1) is now fully closed** — typecheck ✅, build ✅, 24-part visual refs ✅ (28/31, 3 pre-existing FAILs), console ✅, bench ✅ 24/24. Wave 1 unblocked. |
